18

Procedures 

 Preliminary Approval Pre-filing Meeting 

1. Refers an organizing group to this section of the Comptroller's Manual for Corporate Activities. 

2. Instructs the organizing group that it must review this section and become aware of the policies and procedures to charter a national bank. 

NOTE: Unless specific reference is made otherwise, "national bank" throughout these procedures also refers to national trust companies, bankers' banks, and limited purpose banks (such as CEBA credit card banks). 

3. May provide additional information to the organizing group regarding the pre-filing meeting and the parts of the application that must be filed. 

The following criteria generally will determine the parts of the application that each group should file:

Groups that are not requesting streamlined procedures file the entire application.

Existing bank holding companies and independent organizing groups requesting streamlined procedures file the application cover letter including the certification, the application including operating plan, Parts I, II, III, IV, VII, proforma balance sheet and income and expense projections, capital computation, and the applicable parts of the biographical and financial reports. 

NOTE: Foreign nationals and persons with substantial international dealings, required to file biographical and financial reports, must also file certified statements from officials in the applicable foreign country, attesting to the person's integrity and financial capacity (see Applica tions and Notices, Standard Sample Document, Foreign Certifications). 

• Groups that only plan to exercise fiduciary powers need not submit the information requested in Part III of the operating plan on CRA. 

However, if existing trust operations will be merged into the new trust company or form the basis for the de novo trust company's operations, copies of the latest three years' trust call reports are required as part of the application. If applicable, a legal opinion addressing the criteria for qualifying as a trust company under the Competitive 

Equality Banking Act of 1987 (P.L. 100-86) (CEBA) should be sub mitted with the application. If a national trust bank will accept deposits, FDIC insurance is required. The proposal should clearly state if deposits are not anticipated to prevent the OCC from notifing the FDIC for deposit insurance. • Groups interested in organizing a national credit card bank that satisfies the criteria enumerated in CEBA must file a legal opinion addressing compliance with that criteria. In some instances, a firm qualifying as a nonbank holding company under federal law would be considered a bank holding company (BHC) under state law. In such instances, holding company activities permitted by federal law may be prohibited by state law. Consequently, applicants for CEBA 19

Procedures nonbank charters should demonstrate that their applications meet all standards imposed by both federal and state law. 

Applications to charter national banks which pose potential Glass- Steagall concerns (12 U.S.C. 24(7), 78, 377, and 378) require the filing of the entire application plus a legal opinion addressing the potential Glass-Steagall concerns. The district office or Multinational 

Banking (MNB) will provide guidance on the information necessary in the legal opinion at the pre-filing meeting. 

Organizers of a national bankers' bank should file: 

Any agreements (voting trust, service, management, funding, etc.).

Operating plan (all parts except part III). 

Biographical reports on the organizers, directors, and executive officers. For those persons currently known to the OCC through their affiliation with a national bank, only the certification page, the general information, education, employment and miscellaneous information questions 1 and 2 sections of the biographical report are required. For those persons not affiliated with a national bank, or not known to the OCC, entire biographical and financial reports must be submitted. 

Most recent quarter and year-end Reports of Condition and Income and Expenses for each investing/organizing depository institution.

If several organizing banks are affiliates of the same holding company, the most recent annual report and 10K must be submitted.

If the proposed activities pose potential legal concerns, the application should include a legal opinion that discusses the applicability of those concerns and their legality. 

Requests for waivers of statutes, laws, or regulations require submission of a detailed analysis justifying the waiver request. • Requests to expand the powers of an existing limited or special purpose bank to a full service bank via an amendment to the articles of association do not require an application. The bank files a letter requesting OCC approval of the proposed amendments and submits the appropriate filing fee. The limited or special purpose bank must meet the publication and 30-day public comment requirements in 12 

CFR 5.8 and 5.10 (see Documents-Applicants, Legal Notice-Ex pansion of Powers, New Bank Charters). The request letter must be accompanied by a legal opinion concerning state law and its applicability to the proposal and by an abbreviated operating plan. The plan should include: 

A brief description of the market to be served and the services to be offered.

Pro forma balance sheet and income statements accompanied by a brief explanation of assumptions. 

Proposed management and board. 

A CRA statement and notice. 20

Procedures

DDA/DCMNB 

Organizers

NOTE: If the special purpose bank has not been previously insured by the FDIC, the FDIC's comments will be requested regarding the proposal, because all full service banks must be FDIC-insured. 

NOTE: The OCC reserves the right to request additional information from any organizing group. 

4. Holds a pre-filing meeting with the organizing group to review the requirements and procedures for organizing a new bank. If the DDA or 

DCMNB waives the pre-filing meeting, the case file should note the reason.

NOTE: If members of the organizing groups are not present or if the organizers exhibit little or no familiarity with the instructions or application, the OCC representative may adjourn the pre-filing meeting and require the organizers to reschedule when they are better prepared. 

NOTE: At the pre-filing meeting, a member of the organizing group must be identified as spokesperson to represent the organizers in all subsequent contacts with the OCC. The spokesperson must be a proposed director of the new national bank. 

NOTE: For a banker's bank proposal, any proposed waivers or exemptions from statutes or regulations should be disclosed and discussed at the pre-filing meeting. 

5. Presides over the pre-filing meeting (see Documents-OCC, Pre-filing 

Meeting Guidelines, New Bank Charters) and documents the meeting with a memorandum to the file. 

Filing the Application 6. Submit one original and two copies of the appropriate parts of the application and the filing fee as specified in the Annual Notice of the Comptroller of the Currency Fees. Make check payable to the Comptroller of the Currency and deliver the fee with the application by hand, or by mail, return receipt requested, to the district office that has supervisory authority in the location in which the new bank will operate. 

Filing fees are not refunded. 

NOTE: Applications for holding company sponsored bank charters should be filed with the district that has, or will have, primary supervisory authority over the banking organization. Multinational banking companies and their affiliates file with the DCMNB in Washington, D.C. 

NOTE: When fiduciary powers, a branch or a CBCT request is requested with a charter application, an additional filing fee in the appropriate amount is required. 

NOTE: The OCC expects applicants requesting approval for activities or actions involving precedential, unusual, or complex legal issues to research the issues carefully before filing and provide the appropriate written analysis in support of the proposed activity as part of their filing with the OCC. 21

Procedures 7. Designate a member of the organizing group as the spokesperson to whom the OCC will direct all communications regarding the application. 

NOTE: If the name, address, or telephone number of the spokesperson changes during the time the application is pending, notify the OCC immediately so that communication with the group is not interrupted. 

8. State in the cover letter to the application which parts of the application the group has not filed and the reasons why (see step 3). 

NOTE: If at the pre-filing meeting the DDA/DCMNB informed the organizers that they appear to be qualified for streamlined procedures, the application must still address the reasons why the group qualifies for such processing. 

NOTE: Prior to filing, organizers planning to pursue a voting trust or voting agreement must consult with the district office or MNB to discuss requirements for biographical and financial reports. 

NOTE: The proposed CEO must complete the biographical and financial reports whether or not the person is identified in the application. If that person is not identified, all questions should be answered except any which disclose identifying information. Identifying information generally is considered to include the person's name, address, social security number, the names of the financial institutions where the person is or was employed, other organizations where the person is or was affiliated as an officer, and other information which, by its nature, would readily identify the person.

NOTE: For a bankers' bank, the OCC must review and not object to the organizers, proposed CEO and other executive officers. Information as detailed in step 3 pertaining to bankers' banks proposals should be submitted at the time of filing.

Publication Requirements 

9. Publish a notice of the application on the same day the application is sent by registered mail, return receipt requested, or delivered by hand to the OCC. Publication should be in a newspaper of general circulation in the community in which the proposed bank will be located (see 12 

CFR 5.8) (see Documents-Applicant, Legal Notice-Charter Applica tion, New Bank Charters). Banks to be established by an existing bank holding company normally are exempt from this notice requirement because they are subject to the Federal Reserve Board's public notice requirements (see 12 CFR 262.3(b)(1)). However, the OCC will be informed by the Federal Reserve of any negative comments resulting from the holding company notice.

NOTE: If an application must be resubmitted to the OCC because of technical corrections, or if it is amended by the organizers (see Applications and Notices, New Bank Charters, Application-Amendments), the OCC may reopen and extend the public comment period or require republication based on the significance of the amendments. 

NOTE: Significant amendments or changes to the application other than those necessary to make the application technically complete may 22

Procedures cause the OCC to conclude that the original application is obsolete. In such cases, the original application will be considered withdrawn and a new application and filing fee will be required. If a new application is not required, but amendments or changes result in substantial additional review and processing by the OCC, an additional fee may be imposed.

Processing the Application 

10. Reviews the application for accuracy and completeness (see Documents OCC, Processing Checklist, New Bank Charters). Minor corrections will be handled over the telephone. If numerous technical corrections are required, a letter is sent to the organizers detailing the necessary corrections (see Documents-OCC, Information Request, Standard Sample Document). The need for those corrections may cause the application to be returned. Some requests for additional information (such as specific site locations or changes in capital amounts) may necessitate republication.

NOTE: The organizers are generally provided with one opportunity to make technical corrections. If corrections are not made by the group, the application may be considered withdrawn if adequate corrections are not made within a reasonable time. Filing fees are not refunded. 

Subsequent refilings will require a new application, publication, and filing fee.

NOTE: This review only determines if the required parts of the application have been submitted. No substantive analysis of the proposal to charter the new national bank is done at this time. 

11. Initiates data entry into the Licensing Information System (LIS). Checks the Weekly Bulletin for accuracy and completeness and contacts the 

LP&S technicians via EMC2/TAO to report Weekly Bulletin omissions or corrections. 

12. Establishes the official file, called the charter file, as the administrative record to maintain all original documents relating to the application. The file includes the Processing Checklist and the Interested Parties Check list which are used to track the application's review and the notices sent to interested parties of actions involving the application (see Docu ments-OCC, Processing Checklist (New Bank Charters) and Interested Parties Checklist (Standard Sample Document)). 

13. Establishes a public file to maintain the parts of the application that are not confidential and any comments received from the public on the application. The public file will be available to all who inquire about the application.

NOTE: A public file need not be established, unless comments are filed by interested parties, or requests for copies of the application are received.

NOTE: Contacts the Congressional Liaison in Washington whenever a written or telephone inquiry is received from a member of Congress or his or her representative. All congressional inquiries must be documented in the case file. 23

Procedures

Interested Parties DDA/MNB 

NOTE: Do not forward applications for an uninsured trust company charter to the FDIC for comment. If a currently uninsured trust company applies to expand to full commercial services, a copy of the information is sent to the FDIC for comment.

NOTE: If the application is amended, forwards a copy of the amendments to the FDIC (see Documents-OCC, FDIC Notification-Amendments, New Bank Charters). The FDIC has 30 days to provide its original comments. The FDIC may change its comments based on an amendment and, if comments are received prior to a decision, they should be considered. Decisions should not be delayed solely because FDIC's comments have not been received, unless the district gets concurrence from  (BOS). 

14. Forwards the application fee with a deposit memorandum (OCC Form 

6043-01) to P.O. Box 73150, Chicago, IL 60673-7150. 

15. Forwards a copy of the application to the applicable Federal Deposit 

Insurance Corporation (FDIC) regional office for comment pursuant to the Financial Institutions Reform, Recovery, and Enforcement Act of 1989 (FIRREA) (see Documents-OCC, FDIC Notification-Applica tion, New Bank Charters).

Public Comments and Hearings 

16. May comment on the proposal or request a hearing during the 30 days after the notice is published. 

NOTE: In the case of cross district or interstate filings, the comment period may be extended by the OCC. 

17. If a hearing is requested during the 30-day comment period, follows the hearing procedures in the Public Hearings section of this manual. 

NOTE: Requests for public hearings based on CRA concerns or state or federal law should be discussed by the district with BOS. 

Application Review 18. Once the application is technically complete, proceeds with an indepth review and analysis of the application. 

NOTE: The decision to approve or disapprove the application will be based on evaluation of the prospects for success of the proposed new national bank. The evaluation will be based primarily on the information contained in the application and on other information available to the 

OCC as a result of its supervisory activities. 

19. Reviews the qualifications of the organizing group for streamlined procedures when they are requested. 

NOTE: If organizers request streamlined procedures, the file must contain a decision whether to grant or deny them and the reason for the decision. 24

Procedures 20. For bankers' banks, reviews requests for exemptions or waivers from statute or regulation. All such requests must include an analysis justifying the request.

NOTE: The advice of interested OCC divisions must be obtained on requests for exemptions or waivers. At a minimum, the Law Department generally reviews such requests. If organizers request exemptions or waivers, the file must contain a decision by a designated official whether to grant or deny them and the reason for the decision. 

21. Consults with BOS in Washington if the application contains unusual proposals. Consults with Corporate Activity (CA) if information from the 

State Historic Preservation Officer indicates that the proposed site of the new bank may adversely affect a historic property. 

22. Reviews the qualifications and financial condition of the organizing group and the proposed chief executive officer (CEO). The following characteristics should be present: • Proposed directors meet all statutory requirements. 

NOTE: Requests for citizenship waivers are processed in accordance with the Citizenship Waiver Request section of this manual. 

A majority of the proposed directors have banking or business experience. • The proposed directors are knowledgeable about the market and the services to be provided and have ties in the community. • The proposed directors have diverse experience and backgrounds. • The proposed directors and CEO are knowledgeable about the application and will participate actively in the organization and operation of the bank. In bankers' bank proposals, all participating banks should be familiar with the application and operating plan. • The proposed CEO must have the background and experience suitable for pursuing the new bank's operating plan. The CEO must be thoroughly familiar with the operating plan. • The principal shareholders and proposed directors have the financial strength to support their investment. In bankers' bank proposals, the principal participating banks must have financial strength to support their investment. • The organizers, proposed directors, and proposed CEO are found, based on background checks or other information available to the 

OCC, to be persons of integrity.

NOTE: Management interlocks among unaffiliated banks are generally prohibited under the Depository Institution Management 

Interlocks Act (12 U.S.C. 3201) (Interlocks Act). However, in some cases, interlocking management relationships may be approved for newly chartered banks for a period of two years provided a request for an exemption from the Interlocks Act is made by the organizers, and the exemption is granted by the OCC. If such a request is made, it should address: • The "excepted interlocks" being pursued. 25

Procedures 

• Bank management's need to operate as a safe and sound competitor in the market it plans to serve. • The skills and qualifications of the proposed management official(s). • The extent to which such skills or qualifications will meet the needs of the new bank. • The organizers' efforts to determine the availability of other persons with skills and qualifications similar to the proposed manager, but whose service would not cause otherwise prohibited interlocks. • The length of the proposed interlocking service. 

NOTE: Management interlocks are permitted for bankers' banks under 

12 CFR 26.4(b). 

23. Performs background checks on all organizers, the proposed CEO, if the person is identified at the time of the application, any other executive officers, directors, and principal shareholders identified in the application. Background checks on persons currently affiliated with a national bank and involved in a bankers' bank proposal may be waived. All requests for background information (including fingerprint cards) should contain the application control number and request that the responding agency reference the control number in its response. 

NOTE: Background checks will be performed on the proposed CEO and all executive officers and principal shareholders after preliminary approval, when the CEO is given confidential treatment and/or the persons are not disclosed in the application. Bank holding company sponsored applications may be exempted from the background check requirement. If background checks are waived by the DDA/DCMNB, the action should be documented in the case file.

In those instances where there is a conflict between the OCC's need to verify an person's qualifications and a request that a present employer not be contacted, the issue should be resolved in favor of the OCC's need to know. If, however, sufficient information appears to be available through alternative sources, it might not be necessary to contact present employers in all cases. The person should not be given assurances that such contacts will not be made. If contact with present employers will be made, the person should be notified prior to contacting the employer. 

Background checks provide information on the competence, experience, integrity, and financial ability of each person named in the application and provides an independent determination of the accuracy and completeness of the information submitted. Information obtained by the OCC from the background investigation may be shared with the other financial institutions regulatory agencies. Standard background checks have been adopted by the Federal Financial Regulatory Examination Council and must be performed for all organizers, proposed CEOS, executive officers, directors, and principal shareholders (see 

Policy Statements section of this manual, "Background Check and Change in Control Investigation Guidelines.") 

Standard background checks must be made through the: • Federal Bureau of Investigation (FBI). 26

Procedures 

• United States Custom Service (USCS). 

• Drug Enforcement Administration (DEA). 

(ECIS) records search. ECIS includes a database of persons named in OCC administrative actions or criminal referrals.

Persons involved in insider abuse or other situations indicating potential unsuitability for involvement in the affairs of a national bank are also included on ECIS. District Counsel should be consulted on accessing these systems. In addition, Enforcement and Compliance (E&C) will review various printouts provided by the other regulatory agencies which list names of persons against whom enforcement actions have been taken and/or about whom criminal referrals were filed. Contact E&C to determine if a particular person appears on another agency's referral list.

NOTE: The ECIS and LEXIS-NEXIS systems must be accessed to verify whether a person has any history of court cases, litigation, etc. 

Consult with District Counsel for access to those systems. The OCC's librarian will check local newspapers of organizers, proposed CEOS, and principal shareholders, if requested. When these persons are not nationally or regionally prominent, local newspapers may contain information not found in a major paper. If adverse or derogatory information is disclosed from any checked source, determine its significance to the overall filing, and if it is significant, include it in the confidential memorandum. • Federal regulatory agencies that have regulatory jurisdiction over the institutions in which any of the persons or holding company are involved (the FRB, FDIC, and OTS/FHLB). • State depository institution regulatory agencies in those cases where any of the persons or holding companies have been involved with institutions under a state's regulatory jurisdiction. 

NOTE: If, in individual circumstances, any of the standard background checks are not performed because the check would be redundant, does not serve the OCC's purpose, or for any other reason, the file must be adequately documented with the reasons for the waiver. 

In addition to the standard background checks, discretionary checks appropriate to the persons' circumstances should be conducted. Such checks may include, but are not limited to, requests for background information from the Internal Revenue Service (IRS), Securities Ex change Commission (SEC), Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC), National Association of Securities Dealers (NASD), INTERPOL, the International Monetary Fund (IMF), the World Bank, the U.S.

Immi gration and Naturalization Service, the Central Intelligence Agency 

(CIA), the U.S. State Department, and local law enforcement agencies. 

NOTE: All U.S. citizens and all foreign nationals are subject to six mandatory background checks. In addition, for foreign nationals, the 

OCC will contact the OCC Enforcement and Compliance Division, Interpol, the State Department, the CIA, and bank regulators in the persons' home country or other countries where they borrow, own, operate, or manage banks. These checks also may be performed for 27

Procedures 

U.S. citizens, but they are not mandatory. Mandatory checks may be waived, but any waivers must be documented in the case file and must include a brief statement of the basis for the waiver. 

NOTE: All foreign nationals must include with their filing certified statements from local officials attesting to the person's integrity and financial capacity (see Applications and Notices, Standard Sample Document, Foreign Certifications). 

NOTE: U.S. district and state court clerks may be contacted regarding decisions at the trial level in those instances where it is deemed appropriate. Credit checks are performed on acquiring parties when deemed appropriate.

NOTE: The OCC may require additional information to make an independent determination of the accuracy and completeness of any information contained in a charter application. This may include a requirement to submit fingerprints if they are required to access information necessary for a background investigation. 

When fingerprints are required, the OCC will: • Distribute one fingerprint card to each person required to obtain fingerprints. • Instruct the person in writing that the provision of fingerprints is his/her responsibility and is required for the OCC to investigate the party's background thoroughly. • Provide a list of locations convenient to the person where fingerprinting services are available (i.e., the local police or law enforcement office or post offices). • Allow at least 10 business days for fingerprint cards to be returned to the DDA.

The DDA will forward completed fingerprint cards to  to the attention of the Director for Corporate Activity for processing.

NOTE: If adverse information is received from the FBI on a person applying for an organizer, director or executive officer position of a new national bank, the person must be notified and afforded the opportunity to complete, correct or challenge the information prior to the OCC making a decision. The person must be instructed to refer to 28 CFR 

16.34 for guidance and to provide the OCC with a written response within 15 days of receipt of notification (see Documents-OCC, FBI 

Adverse Information, Standard Sample Document). 

If the adverse information is received from a source other than the FBI, the person must still be notified and afforded the opportunity to include additional facts germane to the issue in question. This information must be considered when making a decision.

NOTE: A decision to deny the charter or employment with the new bank based on information in the FBI's record cannot be made until the applicant has been permitted to respond to the information. 28

District Counsel 

24. Reviews the operating plan if the organizing group and the proposed chief executive officer are found to be satisfactory. If deficiencies with the organizing group or proposed chief executive officer are such that they cannot be offset by a satisfactory operating plan, the plan need not be reviewed. NOTE: The letter informing organizers of the decision will indicate whenever a denial was based on deficiencies in the organizing group or proposed CEO and whether the operating plan was reviewed. 

NOTE: For holding company sponsored applications, the review will consider the consolidated entity, including any affiliates' condition. 

NOTE: The operating plan and pro formas should be reviewed with consideration given to a range of possible favorable and unfavorable market conditions. The effects of competition in the new bank's market area must be addressed. 

25. Sends the application for review to other OCC staff, if necessary, including legal counsel, the field office director, Special Supervision, Regional Banking, Trust Activities, another district office, or Consumer

Activities. Requests a memo for the file, when necessary. Applicants are encouraged to prepare and submit a thorough legal review of any proposals raising significant legal issues. If district legal staff determines that a legal review of sufficient scope was not submitted initially, District Counsel will request an additional opinion as soon as it is determined to be ncecssary. 

26. Determines legal delegations as they apply to licensing matters. District 

Counsel or staff informs licensing, either in a cover memo or at the beginning of the legal opinion, whether the relevant legal issues, if any, are delegated or not.

NOTE: District legal opinions and relevant background materials are forwarded to the district licensing division rather than directly to LASD or another Washington legal division. This insures that licensing and legal information is combined in one official file before that file is forwarded to BOS, if applicable. 

27. Requests a field investigation or additional review which generally should be completed within 60 days of the request (see Documents- 

OCC, Field Investigation Request and Field Investigation Notification 

(New Bank Charters)). Forwards instructions for the field investigation to the examiner (see Documents-OCC, Field Investigation Instructions and Field Investigation Guidelines (New Bank Charters)). 

NOTE: Field investigations are recommended for all bank charter applications not filed under streamlined procedures. However, field investigations should be targeted as much as possible. If the field investigation is waived, reasons for doing so must be documented in the file. 

28. Prepares a confidential memorandum and a confidential memorandum checklist and sends them for decision to the Deputy Comptroller (District) (DCD), or Deputy Comptroller  in the case of multinationals, (see Documents-OCC, Confidential 29

Procedures 

Deputy Comptroller (District) (DCD)/DCMNB 

DDA/DCMNB Memorandum and Confidential Memorandum Checklist (New Bank 

Charters)).

NOTE: Completion of the "Biographical and Financial Reports-Sum mary Worksheet" may facilitate preparation of the confidential memorandum (see Documents-OCC, Biographical and Financial Reports Summary Worksheet, New Bank Charters). 

Decision 29. Makes a decision under delegated authority or refers the proposal to Washington.

Preliminary Approval. If the OCC has no objections to the plans to organize a bank, issues a letter of preliminary approval with standard conditions and in-organization documents (see Documents-OCC, Approval/Conditional Approval Letter, New Bank Charters). 

NOTE: The standard conditions may be expanded based on the type of charter requested (i.e., trust company, CEBA credit card bank, or bankers' bank).

Applications to organize a national bank normally will be approved if: • The organizing group and proposed CEO have satisfactory personal and business backgrounds. For a bankers' bank, the organizing group of banks are in satisfactory condition. • The market analysis is well documented and reasonable for the market. • Plans and objectives are accurate and realistic. • Proposed capital is adequate for the market and products, and plans to raise capital are realistic. In holding company situations, no reliance on cash flows from the new bank are required during initial years of operation to service any debt acquired to capitalize the bank. • Plans to hire additional personnel for the bank are consistent with the objectives of the operating plan. • The strength of the local economy as it relates to the operating plan, the organizing group, and the proposed chief executive officer (or qualifications if not identified) are satisfactory.

NOTE: The organizers may begin organizing the bank as soon as they receive notification from the OCC that their articles of association and organization certificate are accepted by the OCC (see step 46). However, the business of banking may not be commenced until a charter is issued.

NOTE: Final approval will not be given to holding company banks until the holding company has been approved by the Federal Reserve. 

30. If the decision is to approve the application, performs the following actions: 30

• Notifies all interested parties (see Documents-OCC, Interested

31. Makes data entries into the Licensing Information System (LIS) to note preliminary approval. Checks the Weekly Bulletin for accuracy and completeness and contacts the LP&S technicians via EMC2/TAO to report Weekly Bulletin omissions or corrections. 

NOTE: A copy of any notice to a member of Congress must be sent to the OCC's Congressional Liaison in Washington on or before the date it is mailed to Congress. If, prior to a decision, a congressional inquiry or advisement of a congressional inquiry has been received by telephone, the district should inform the Congressional Liaison of the decision, as soon as it is made. 

32. If the DCD refers the application to Washington, sends the complete official file, including the confidential memorandum, to Bank Organiza tion and Structure (BOS). Makes appropriate LIS entries. 

NOTE: If the file is forwarded to BOS, the file should note whether any legal issues in the case are considered delegated or nondelegated to district counsel. Additionally, if special conditions are proposed, the district must indicate in the file whether they recommend the use of 12 

U.S.C. 1818 language in the approval letter.

NOTE: In corporate application approvals, references to the enforceability of written conditions under 12 U.S.C. 1818(b)(1) generally will be limited to approvals containing special conditions that remain in effect after the effective date or consummation date of an approved transaction or activity.

Recommends a decision. Forwards the file to the appropriate 

Washington office decision-maker. 

34. Takes one of the following actions on the application: • Preliminary approval (see steps 29-31). • Conditional preliminary approval. The OCC has specific concerns about the organizing group, the proposed chief executive officer, or the operating plan which it believes can be corrected during the bank-in-organization stage. Therefore, the OCC authorizes the organizing group to begin organizing the bank, noting that a charter will not be issued until the conditions outlined by the OCC are satisfied (see Approval/Conditional Approval Letter). • Denial. Organizers failed to demonstrate that the proposed bank had a reasonable likelihood of success (see Documents-OCC, Disapproval Letter, New Bank Charters). 31

BOS 

35. If the decision is to approve or conditionally approve the application, performs the following actions: • Notifies the DDA or DCMNB by telephone of the decision and provides appropriate feedback on the analysis and decision. • Transmits decision to spokesperson, requests that the organization fee be paid within 30 days and directs organizers to the district for the in-organization phase. • Notifies interested parties, if applicable. • Makes data entries into the Licensing Information System (LIS) to note preliminary approval. Checks the Weekly Bulletin for accuracy and completeness and contacts the LP&S technicians via EMC2/TAO to report Weekly Bulletin omissions or corrections. • If conditionally approved, sends a copy of the decision and feedback sheet to the LP&S quality control coordinator. • Returns the official file with all pertinent material to the district or MNB. 

NOTE: If an organizer, proposed director or management candidate is disapproved, but the proposal is considered to still have a likelihood for success, a copy of the letter objecting to the person must also be sent to him/her. 

36. If the decision is to deny the application, perform the following actions: • Notifies the DDA or DCMNB by telephone of the decision and forwards a copy of the confidential memo, feedback sheet, and decision letter. • Notifies the spokesperson by telephone and by letter. • If a reason for denial is based on the background investigation of an organizer, proposed director or management candidate, a copy of the denial letter must be sent to the person. • Notifies interested parties, if applicable.

NOTE: A copy of any notice to a member of Congress must be sent to the OCC's Congressional Liaison in Washington on or before the date it is mailed to Congress. If, prior to a decision, a congressional inquiry or advisement of a congressional inquiry had been received by telephone, BOS should inform the Congressional Liaison of the decision, as soon as it is made. • Makes data entries into LIS to note denial. Checks the Weekly Bulletin for accuracy and completeness and contacts the LP&S technicians via EMC2/TAO to report Weekly Bulletin omissions or corrections. • Sends a copy of the decision and feedback sheet to the LP&S quality control coordinator. • Closes out the file and retains it in BOS. After six months, forwards the file to Central Records (CR). 32

Procedures 

Organizers

DDA/DCMNB 

Organizing the Bank 

37. Proceed promptly with the organization of the bank as proposed in the application.

NOTE: The organizing group must notify the district office when potential significant changes are planned or occur. District staff should be alert to a series of small changes occurring over extended periods of time. 

These may be significant when considered together. They may also suggest a pattern of performance by organizers that could cause the OCC to question its confidence in the organizing group's ability to successfully operate a new bank. District staff also should be attentive to potential changes signalled by information in offering circulars that differ from that filed in the original approved application. 

Based on a review of changes, a district may decide to allow the existing preliminary approval or preliminary conditional approval to stand. No additional action is required other than pertinent file documentation of the change, district views, and discussions with the applicant. LIS also must be updated as necessary.

Some proposed changes may be so significant that they require additional substantive analysis of the charter proposal. Then, the districts may require applicants to republish and/or remit an additional fee. The 

Director, Licensing Policy and Systems, may be consulted regarding republication or an additional fee. 

The district review of proposed changes may result in a recommendation for rescission of the original approval, or for new special conditions to be attached to the original approval. In such cases, the district's analysis and recommendation, with pertinent supporting documentation, should be forwarded to  (BOS) for decision. Prior to forwarding to BOS, the district should log the significant change onto LIS, using the miscellaneous processes classification code, and cross reference the control numbers in the LIS comments sections.

NOTE: A change may be considered significant yet may also enhance the proposal. Significant changes are not automatically viewed as negative or detrimental to the original proposal. Therefore, organizers are encouraged to submit all proposed changes during the in-organization process to the OCC for prior review. Discovery by the OCC of changes later in the process could delay final approval or result in revocation of preliminary approval.

NOTE: Generally, previously granted approvals will be revoked if the 

OCC becomes aware of material violations of law, misrepresentations, or any fraudulent activity on the part of the applicants. In addition, revocation may occur due to an adverse change in the condition of a sponsoring holding company or any of the participating banks involved in a bankers' bank proposal. 

38. Identifies proposed significant changes to a preliminarily approved proposal. Prepares an analysis of the proposed change. 33

Procedures BOS 

Organizers 39. The district informs the spokesperson and the group continues organizing the bank if the district decides a change is not significant, does not negatively affect the proposal, or otherwise warrants a change in the conditions imposed in the preliminary approval letter. 

40. Prepares a recommendation and forwards it to BOS for final decision if the district determines that the proposed change is significant, has already occurred, or adversely affects the proposal, and approving such a change would lead to additional conditions or revocation. 

NOTE: If the proposed change will result in additional conditions or revocation, the district should inform the spokesperson of the OCC's concerns prior to decision and allow the group to alter or withdraw the proposed change. If the change has already occurred and/or is not in the best interests of the bank in organization, district forwards the case to BOS. 

41. Decides if a significant change has occurred and whether additional conditions or revocation of preliminary approval are warranted. 

42. Notifies the district and organizers of the decision. 

NOTE: District and BOS analysts will ensure that appropriate tracking comments appear on LIS to facilitate tracking changes during the organization phase.

NOTE: If the proposed site of the new bank's building will have an adverse effect on a historical property as identified by the State's Historic Preservation Office, final approval may be delayed until the OCC is notified by the State that its concerns have been eliminated. 

Formation of a Body Corporate 43. Review instructions for organizing a new national bank (see Docu ments OCC, Organization Instructions, New Bank Charters). Forwards a copy to the OCC of any filings to the Federal Reserve or SEC, including the offering circular. 

44. Submit prescribed fee to the district office by check payable to the 

Comptroller of the Currency within 30 days of receipt of the preliminary approval letter.

NOTE: Limited and special purpose charters must limit their activities in article two of their articles of association. This limitation applies to trust companies, CEBA credit card banks and bankers' banks (see Documents-Applicant, Articles of Association, Standard Sample Document, sample language for article 2).

Organizing Directors 

46. Records the receipt of payment in the pending file and forwards the remittance with the deposit memorandum form (CC 6043-01) to P.O. 

Box 73150, Chicago, IL 60673-7150. 

47. Reviews the articles of association and organization certificate for compliance with all legal requirements and in conjunction with the processing checklist.

NOTE: 12 CFR 7.5217 provides for the indemnification of directors, officers, and employees to be contained in the bank's articles of association. However, the OCC must review such indemnification articles in accordance with its supervisory responsibilities to assess the potential effects on bank safety and soundness posed by such indemnifications.

NOTE: The OCC also will review all anti-takeover articles for supervisory concerns and for possible violation of national banking laws.

49. Informs the spokesperson for the organizers by telephone, and by letter, that the articles of association and the organization certificate are not in contravention of the law and are accepted by the OCC (see Docu ments-OCC, Acceptance of Articles and Organization Certificate, Standard Sample Document). 

50. Attaches a copy of the articles of association and organization certificate to the letter and places a copy of the letter and each document in the OCC's official file.

52. Keep minutes of the meeting and, if requested, make them available to the OCC (see Documents-Applicant, Minutes of Organizers' Meeting-Minutes and Organizing Board's First Meeting-Minutes (Stand ard Sample Documents)).

Establishing Management 

59. Submit to the OCC the biographical and financial reports and background checks on each candidate for an executive officer position for whom reports have not previously been filed (see Application and 

Notices, Biographical Report and Financial Report (Standard Sample Documents) and Documents-OCC, Organization Instructions, New 

Bank Charters). Organizing groups experienced-in-banking (see definition in the introduction to this section under "Streamlined Procedures") may be exempted from this requirement. 

NOTE: If the CEO was not named in the application, the person must be disclosed at this time. Any biographical and financial information not previously provided must be submitted. 

NOTE: The president must be employed before the stock is offered, and the cashier, before the charter is granted. The OCC must review and not object to persons proposed for both these positions.

Establishing a Federal Branch or Agency Introduction Summary of the Process 

Foreign banks must receive a license from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) before they can establish a federal branch or agency in the United 

States. The licensing process consists of three phases: 

prefiling discussions; filing the application; and organizing the federal branch or agency. Prefiling meetings are conducted by the OCC's International Banking and 

Finance Division in Washington, D.C. to discuss OCC 

licensing policies and filing requirements. Upon request, the OCC will waive the prefiling meeting for foreign banks that have previous banking experience in the U.S.

After the prefiling meeting, the foreign bank files an application and the standard filing fee. The application must include: • Financial statements. • General information about the applicant bank, such as its ownership, corporate structure, and home country supervision. • An operating plan for the proposed federal branch or agency. • Biographical reports on proposed management. • Legal opinions certifying that the U.S. operation will be conducted in compliance with domestic and foreign laws. • A Community Reinvestment Act Statement if deposits of less than $100,000 will be accepted. 

If a foreign bank wishes to exercise fiduciary powers at a federal branch, it must obtain OCC approval in accordance with 12 CFR 5.26. A letter of intent to exercise fiduciary powers may be submitted at the time of filing for a federal branch, license or at any subsequent date. 

Generally, a fiduciary powers request will be decided at the same time as the federal branch application. 

However, a fiduciary powers permit will not be issued until the federal branch opens. The federal branch can begin to exercise those powers, when it receives the permit.

If the federal branch wishes to accept deposits under $100,000, a separate application for insurance must be filed with the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation. 

The OCC performs an analysis of the application and makes a decision on the proposed federal branch or agency following receipt of a complete application. 

Application processing may be delayed because of a public hearing request; a special analysis or investigation; or an additional information request. If the proposal is satisfactory, the bank is given preliminary approval to establish the federal branch or agency according to the plan described in the application. The federal branch or agency must commence business within 18 months from the date of the preliminary approval. Generally, the OCC will not extend the 18-month period. 

Following preliminary approval, the foreign bank establishes its banking premises at the proposed site; establishes its capital equivalency deposit account; and develops its U.S. banking systems. At least two weeks before the federal branch or agency is scheduled to open, the OCC conducts a review to verify that it has met all requirements for commencing the business of banking in the U.S. If the federal branch or agency has met the requirements for opening, a license is granted. 

If the examination uncovers numerous exceptions or significant deviations from the originally approved proposal, the opening may be delayed or the original preliminary approval may be revoked. Background

Federal branches and agencies are linked closely to foreign parent banks because they are merely extensions in the U.S. of those banks and have no separate legal identity. Prior to 1978, foreign banks desiring to open a branch or agency in the U.S. could do so only under state law. The passage of the International Banking Act of 1978 allowed foreign banks to: • Open a federal branch or agency. • Convert an existing state branch, agency, or commercial lending company into a federal branch or agency. • Establish additional federal branches or agencies. • Relocate a federal branch or agency.

Redesignate an initial federal branch to an agency and vice versa. Definitions

A federal branch is an office or place of business licensed by the OCC and operated in the U.S. by a foreign bank. It can engage in the business of banking, exercise fiduciary powers, and accept deposits. 

A federal agency is an office or place of business licensed by the OCC and operated in the U.S. by a foreign bank. It can engage in the business of banking, but cannot exercise fiduciary powers or accept deposits. However, a federal agency may maintain credit balances that are incidental to or arising out of the exercise of banking powers; are not intended to be deposits; and do not remain in the receiving institution 42

Establishing a Federal Branch or Agency Introduction after the transaction(s) to which they relate is completed.

A limited federal branch is a federal branch licensed by the OCC which, pursuant to an agreement between the parent foreign bank and the Federal Reserve Board, can receive only such deposits as would be permissible for an Edge Corporation organized under Section 

25(a) of the Federal Reserve Act (12 U.S.C. 611). 

Except for this restriction, a limited federal branch can exercise the full range of powers available to any federal branch.

Capital equivalency deposits are deposits required for both federal branches and agencies. Upon the opening of a federal branch or agency, a foreign bank must have dollar deposits or investment securities on deposit with a member bank. The deposit arrangements and any subsequent reductions must be approved by the OCC. 

The aggregate amount of deposited dollars and investment securities for each federal branch or agency shall, at a minimum, equal the greater of: (1) the amount of capital that would be required of a national bank being organized at the same location; or, (2) 5 percent of the total liabilities of the federal branch or agency. The OCC may require in individual cases that the capital equivalency deposits be increased to conform to general accepted banking practices. The depository institution must be located in the same state as the federal branch or agency and be approved by the OCC, if it is a national bank, or by the Federal Reserve Board, if it is a state member bank. Funds deposited and investment securities placed in safekeeping at the depository bank to satisfy the capital equivalency requirements of the foreign bank shall be pledged to the Comptroller. 

Key Policies 

The policies and procedures in this section cover requests by foreign banks only to establish federal branches and agencies in the U.S. Foreign banks should contact the Deputy Comptroller for International 

Banking and Finance in Washington for information, if they want to: • Establish additional branches. • Convert any state branch or agency or commercial lending company to a federal branch or agency. • Relocate a federal branch or agency. • Change the designation of its initial federal branch or agency.

Except as otherwise provided by the International Banking Act of 1978 or other federal laws or regulations, a federal branch is authorized to operate under the same rights and privileges and shall be subject to the same duties, restrictions, penalties, liabilities, conditions and limitations that would apply to a national bank at the same location. Factors the OCC must consider in deciding whether to approve or disapprove applications to operate a federal branch or agency in the U.S. include the: • Effect of the proposed branch or agency on competition in the domestic and foreign commerce of the 

• Financial and managerial resources and future prospects of the applicant foreign bank and the proposed branch or agency. • Convenience and needs of the community to be served. • Condition of the foreign banks. • Extent of home country supervision. • Economic stability in the home country. • Reasonableness of the operating plan. • Quality of proposed management. • Legality of the proposal.

The first three factors are statutory considerations. 

The OCC neither ensures that a proposal is without risk, nor protects existing competitors from the competition a new federal branch or agency may provide. The agency believes that federal branches and agencies in the U.S. contribute to domestic and foreign commerce and benefit the locales in which they are established by promoting trade and other international and domestic transactions. Accordingly, unless information in a specific application reveals the possibility of an adverse effect on commerce, competition, or community convenience and needs, the statutory factors will be deemed to be positive.

The OCC generally will condition or deny the application if: • The federal branch or agency will have an adverse effect on commerce, competition, or the community. • The parent bank does not have a record of financially and managerially sound operations. • The parent bank has an unsatisfactory record in U.S. related banking transactions. • The parent bank has not developed a reasonable plan for sound and successful operation of the federal branch or agency. 43

Establishing a Federal Branch or Agency Introduction 

• The primary home country supervisor recommends that the application be denied. • The parent bank is headquartered in a country that lacks an adequate degree of bank regulatory supervision. • Other terms or conditions are deemed appropriate by the OCC.

Capital Computations 

Federal branches and agencies are required to abide by the same laws, rulings, and regulations as any U.S. commercial bank with a national bank charter. Included are limitations and restrictions on certain activities that are based on a percentage of capital. Any limitation or restriction based upon the capital stock and surplus of a national bank shall be deemed to refer, as applied to a federal branch or agency, to the dollar equivalent of the capital stock and surplus of the foreign bank. 

General Applicable Procedures 

In general, the OCC does not look favorably on a proposal, or a series of proposals that would materially alter the plans set forth in a preliminarily approved application. If during the organizing phase changes occur which significantly alter plans, the approval may be revoked. Proposed changes that would normally require the filing of an application, such as relocations or fiduciary powers, will be considered as a potential significant change relative to the original proposal. Fees

A filing fee must accompany the application in accordance with 12 CFR 5.5 and the current Notice of the 

Comptroller of the Currency Fees. Filing fees will not be returned if the application is withdrawn or disapproved. 

Publication Requirements 

As required by 12 CFR 5.8, notice of the application must be published in a newspaper of general circulation in the community in which the applicant proposes to establish the federal branch or agency. Interested parties have 30 days from the date of publication of the notice to submit written comments.

Additional Information 

The application must state accurately and fully the facts the OCC needs to determine that the establishment of a proposed federal branch or agency is consistent with law and applicable regulations. The OCC may require additional data at any time during the processing of the application. When additional information is requested, the decision generally will be delayed. If an inadequate response is received to requests for additional information, the application may be considered withdrawn or disapproved.

16. Performs background checks on proposed executive officers and directors identified in the application. All requests for background information (including fingerprint cards) should contain the application control number and request the responding agency reference the control number in their response.

NOTE: Background checks will be performed on the proposed executive officers after preliminary approval, when the executive officers are given confidential treatment and/or the persons are not disclosed in the application.

In those instances where there is a conflict between the OCC's need to verify a person's qualifications and a request that a present employer not be contacted, the issue should be resolved in favor of the OCC's need to know. If, however, sufficient information appears to be available through alternative sources, it might not be necessary to contact present employers in all cases. The person should not be given assurances that such contacts will not be made. If contact with present employers will 48

Establishing a Federal Branch or Agency Procedures be made, the person should be notified prior to contacting the employer. 

Background checks provide information on the competence, experience and integrity of each person named in the application and provides an independent determination of the accuracy and completeness of the information submitted. Information obtained by the OCC from the background investigation may be shared with the other financial institutions regulatory agencies. Standard background checks have been adopted by the Federal Financial Regulatory Examination Council and must be performed for proposed executive officers and directors (see Policy Statements section of this manual for the interagency agreement on background investigations). If any of the standard background checks are waived, the reasons must be documented fully in the case file. 

Standard background checks must be made through the: • Federal Bureau of Investigation (FBI). • United States Custom Service (USCS). 

• Drug Enforcement Administration (DEA). 

(ECIS) records search. ECIS includes a database of persons named in OCC administrative actions or criminal referrals.

Persons involved in insider abuse or other situations indicating potential unsuitability for involvement in the affairs of a national bank are also included on ECIS. District counsel should be consulted on accessing these systems. In addition, Enforcement and Compliance (E&C) will review various printouts provided by the other regulatory agencies which list names of persons against whom enforcement actions have been taken and/or about whom criminal referrals were filed. Contact E&C to determine if a particular person appears on another agency's referral list. • Federal regulatory agencies that have regulatory jurisdiction over the institutions in which any of the persons or holding company are involved (the FRB, FDIC, and OTS/FHLB). • State depository institution regulatory agencies in those cases where any of the persons or holding companies have been involved with institutions under a state's regulatory jurisdiction. 

NOTE: The ECIS and LEXIS-NEXIS systems must be accessed to verify whether a person has any history of court cases, litigation, etc. 

Consult with District Counsel for access to those systems. If adverse or derogatory information is disclosed from any checked source, determine its significance to the overall filing, and if it is significant, include it in the confidential memorandum. 

NOTE: If, in individual circumstances, any of the standard background checks are not performed because the check would be redundant, does not serve the OCC's purpose, or for any other reason, the file must be adequately documented with the reasons for the waiver.

In addition to the standard background checks, discretionary checks appropriate to the persons' circumstances should be conducted. Such 49

Establishing a Federal Branch or Agency Procedures checks may include, but are not limited to, requests for background information from the Internal Revenue Service (IRS), Securities Ex change Commission (SEC), Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC), National Association of Securities Dealers (NASD), INTERPOL, the International Monetary Fund (IMF), the World Bank, the U.S.

Immi gration and Naturalization Service, the Central Intelligence Agency 

(CIA), the U.S. State Department and local law enforcement agencies. 

NOTE: All U.S. citizens and all foreign nationals are subject to six mandatory background checks. In addition, for foreign nationals, the 

OCC will contact the OCC Enforcement and Compliance Division, Interpol, the State Department, the CIA, and bank regulators in the persons' home country or other countries where they borrow, own, operate, or manage banks. These checks also may be performed for 

U.S. citizens, but they are not mandatory. Mandatory checks may be waived, but any waivers must be documented in the case file and must include a brief statement of the basis for the waiver. 

NOTE: All foreign nationals must include with their filing certified statements from local officials attesting to their integrity and financial capacity 

(see Applications and Notices, Standard Sample Document, Foreign 

Certifications). 

17. In instances involving a foreign individual review certified statements submitted from officials in the applicable foreign country attesting to the person's integrity.

Bank Service Corporations Introduction Summary of Process 

A national bank that wishes to invest in a bank service corporation must, in certain circumstances, obtain the approval of the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC). The OCC is informed of the proposed investment in a letter of notification. Two or more national banks wishing to invest in the same bank service corporation may inform the OCC in a single combined notification letter. However, the OCC will consider each bank's request separately. The notification letter describes the name and location of the bank service corporation and the services to be performed. In addition, it must show that the amount to be invested in each bank service corporation is 10 percent or less of each bank's paid-in and unimpaired capital and unimpaired surplus and that the total amount invested in all bank service corporations is 5 percent or less of the total assets of each bank. The letter also must include information addressing the operations of the proposed bank service corporation and their effect on each investing bank. Background

The Bank Service Corporation Act (12 U.S.C. 1861 et seq.

) was amended significantly by Section 709 of the 

Garn-St Germain Depository Institutions Act of 1982 

(Pub. L. 97-320, October 15, 1982). Prior to that time the OCC had no filing requirements for a bank wishing to invest in a bank service corporation. 

Key Policies 

The OCC will consider a national bank's request under 12 U.S.C. 1865(a) to invest in a bank service corporation and make a final decision within 90 days of the date it acknowledges receipt of a technically complete letter requesting approval. If the OCC fails to make a decision within that time, the investment may be deemed approved.

For banks not subject to special supervisory concerns, approval for the investment in the bank service corporation generally will be granted, provided that: • The services performed by the bank service corporation are legally permissible for a national bank. • The services are performed only at locations in the state where the investing national bank(s) could be authorized to perform such services. • The amount of the investment is limited to the 10 and 

5 percent limitations. • The investing banks are all located in the same state. 

For banks subject to special supervisory concerns, the request may be denied or it may be approved subject to conditions considered appropriate by the OCC.
